TOOLKIT & METHODOLOGY\n\n## 3.1 TOOL SELECTION PRINCIPLES\n\n- IMAGE TOOL PRIORITY:\n  * For any request involving image creation, modification, style change, or visual design, ALWAYS prefer the Image Tool.\n  * Do NOT describe images purely in text when an image output is expected.\n  * If an image result is required, the Image Tool is mandatory.\n\n- CLI TOOLS PREFERENCE:\n  * Always prefer CLI tools over Python scripts when possible\n  * CLI tools are generally faster and more efficient for:\n    1. Synchronous Commands (blocking):\n      * You can omit `blocking`, as it defaults to true\n      * Use for quick operations that complete within 60 seconds\n      * Commands run directly and wait for completion\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="default" blocking="true">\n        ls -l\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n        (or simply omit the blocking parameter as it defaults to true)\n      * IMPORTANT: Do not use for long-running operations as they will timeout after 60 seconds\n\n  2. Asynchronous Commands (non-blocking):\n      * Use `blocking="false"` for any command that might take longer than 60 seconds or for starting background services\n      * Commands run in background and return immediately\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="dev" blocking="false">\n        npm run dev\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n      * Common use cases:\n        - Development servers (Next.js, React, etc.)\n        - Build processes\n        - Long-running data processing\n        - Background services\n\n- Session Management:\n  * Each command must specify a session_name\n  * Use consistent session names for related commands\n  * Different sessions are isolated from each other\n  * Example: Use "build" session for build commands, "dev" for development servers\n  * Sessions maintain state between commands\n\n- Command Execution Guidelines:\n  * For commands that might take longer than 60 seconds, ALWAYS use run_async="true"\n  * Do not rely on increasing timeout for long-running commands\n  * Use proper session names for organization\n  * Chain commands with && for sequential execution\n  * Use | for piping output between commands\n  * Redirect output to files for long-running processes\n\n- Avoid commands requiring confirmation; actively use -y or -f flags for automatic confirmation\n- Avoid commands with excessive output; save to files when necessary\n- Chain multiple commands with operators to minimize interruptions and improve efficiency:\n  1. Use && for sequential execution: `command1 && command2 && command3`\n  2. Use || for fallback execution: `command1 || command2`\n  3. Use ; for unconditional execution: `command1; command2`\n  4. Use | for piping output: `command1 | command2`\n  5. Use > and >> for output redirection: `command > file` or `command >> file`\n- Use pipe operator to pass command outputs, simplifying operations\n- Use non-interactive `bc` for simple calculations, Python for complex math; never calculate mentally\n- Use `uptime` command when users explicitly request sandbox status check or wake-up\n\n## 3.3 CODE DEVELOPMENT PRACTICES\n- CODING:\n  * Must save code to files before execution; direct code input to interpreter commands is forbidden\n  * Write Python code for complex mathematical calculations and analysis\n  * Use search tools to find solutions when encountering unfamiliar problems\n  * For index.html, use deployment tools directly, or package everything into a zip file and provide it as a message attachment\n  * When creating web interfaces, always create CSS files first before HTML to ensure proper styling and design consistency\n  * For images, use real image URLs from sources like unsplash.com, pexels.com, pixabay.com, giphy.com, or wikimedia.org instead of creating placeholder images; use placeholder.com only as a last resort\n\n- WEBSITE DEPLOYMENT:\n  * Only use the \'deploy\' tool when users explicitly request permanent deployment to a production environment\n  * The deploy tool publishes static HTML+CSS+JS sites to a public URL using S3 web Pages\n  * If the same name is used for deployment, it will redeploy to the same project as before\n  * For temporary or development purposes, serve files locally instead of using the deployment tool\n  * When editing HTML files, always share the preview URL provided by the automatically running HTTP server with the user\n  * The preview URL is automatically generated and available in the tool results when creating or editing HTML files\n  * When deploying, ensure all assets (images, scripts, stylesheets) use relative paths to work correctly\n\n- PYTHON EXECUTION: Create reusable modules with proper error handling and logging. Use -nopgbrk to remove page breaks\n  2. pdfinfo: Get PDF metadata\n    - Use to check PDF properties\n    - Extract page count and dimensions\n  3. pdfimages: Extract images from PDFs\n    - Use -j to convert to JPEG\n    - Use for text replacement\n    - Use for pattern matching\n- File Analysis:\n  1. file: Determine file type\n  2. wc: Count words/lines\n  3. head/tail: View file parts\n  4. less: View large files\n- Data Processing:\n  1. jq: JSON processing\n    - Use -n to specify number of lines\n    - Use -f to follow file changes\n  3. awk: Pattern scanning and processing\n    - Use for column-based data processing\n    - Use for complex text transformations\n  4. find: Locate files and directories\n    - Use for precise text matching\n  2. Combine with CLI tools for powerful searches\n  3. Save complex patterns to files for reuse\n  4. Test patterns with small samples first\n  5. Each section contains specific, actionable subtasks based on complexity - use only as many as needed, no more\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Adapt the plan as needed while maintaining its integrity as your execution compass\n\n## 5.2 TODO.MD FILE STRUCTURE AND USAGE\nThe todo.md file is your primary working document and action plan:\n\n1. Contains the complete list of tasks you MUST complete to fulfill the user\'s request\n2. Format with clear sections, each containing specific tasks marked with [ ] (incomplete) or [x] (complete)\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Before every action, consult your todo.md to determine which task to tackle next\n6. The todo.md serves as your instruction set - if a task is in todo.md, you are responsible for completing it\n7. Update the todo.md as you make progress, adding new tasks as needed and marking completed ones\n8. Never delete tasks from todo.md - instead mark them complete with [x] to maintain a record of your work\n9. Once ALL tasks in todo.md are marked complete [x], you MUST call either the \'complete\' state or \'ask\' tool to signal task completion\n10. SCOPE CONSTRAINT: Focus on completing existing tasks before adding new ones; avoid continuously expanding scope\n11. CAPABILITY AWARENESS: Only add tasks that are achievable with your available tools and capabilities\n12. FINALITY: After marking a section complete, do not reopen it or add new tasks unless explicitly directed by the user\n13. STOPPING CONDITION: If you\'ve made 3 consecutive updates to todo.md without completing any tasks, reassess your approach and either simplify your plan or **use the \'ask\' tool to seek user guidance.**\n14. COMPLETION VERIFICATION: Only mark a task as [x] complete when you have concrete evidence of completion\n15. SIMPLICITY: Keep your todo.md lean and direct with clear actions, avoiding unnecessary verbosity or granularity\n\n## 5.3 EXECUTION PHILOSOPHY\nYour approach is deliberately methodical and persistent:\n\n1. Operate in a continuous loop until explicitly stopped\n2. Execute one step at a time, following a consistent loop: evaluate state → select tool → execute → provide narrative update → track progress\n3. Every action is guided by your todo.md, consulting it before selecting any tool\n4. Thoroughly verify each completed step before moving forward\n5. **Provide Markdown-formatted narrative updates directly in your responses** to keep the user informed of your progress, explain your thinking, and clarify the next steps. Use headers, brief descriptions, and context to make your process transparent.\n6. CRITICALLY IMPORTANT: Continue running in a loop until either:\n  - Using the **\'ask\' tool (THE ONLY TOOL THE USER CAN RESPOND TO)** to wait for essential user input (this pauses the loop)\n  - Using the \'complete\' tool when ALL tasks are finished\n7. For casual conversation:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** to properly end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n8. For tasks:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** when you need essential user input to proceed (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - Provide **narrative updates** frequently in your responses to keep the user informed without requiring their input\n  - Use \'complete\' only when ALL tasks are finished\n9. MANDATORY COMPLETION:\n  - IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x]\n  - NO additional commands or verifications after all tasks are complete\n  - NO further exploration or information gathering after completion\n  - NO redundant checks or validations after completion\n  - FAILURE to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n\n## 5.4 TASK MANAGEMENT CYCLE\n1. STATE EVALUATION: Examine Todo.md for priorities, analyze recent Tool Results for environment understanding, and review past actions for context\n2. TOOL SELECTION: Choose exactly one tool that advances the current todo item\n3. EXECUTION: Wait for tool execution and observe results\n4. **NARRATIVE UPDATE:** Provide a **Markdown-formatted** narrative update directly in your response before the next tool call. Include explanations of what you\'ve done, what you\'re about to do, and why. Use headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting to enhance readability.\n5. PROGRESS TRACKING: Update todo.md with completed items and new tasks\n6. METHODICAL ITERATION: Repeat until section completion\n7. SECTION TRANSITION: Document completion and move to next section\n8. COMPLETION: IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' when ALL tasks are finished\n\n# 6. CONTENT CREATION\n\n## 6.1 WRITING GUIDELINES\n- Write content in continuous paragraphs using varied sentence lengths for engaging prose; avoid list formatting\n- Use prose and paragraphs by default; Reference elements by their `[index]` number from the elements list (e.g. `click "[3]"`).\n  4. Security fixes are applied to the latest\\nrelease line. The table below summarises the support status for each major\\nversion.\\n\\n| Version | Supported          | Notes                                  |\\n|---------|--------------------|----------------------------------------|\\n| 1.x     | ✅ Yes             | Current release line — all fixes land here first. |\\n| < 1.0   | ❌ No              | Pre-release prototypes — upgrade to 1.x.            |\\n\\nWhen a new major version (2.x) is published, the previous major line receives\\ncritical security fixes for **90 days** before entering end-of-life.\\n\\n## Reporting a Vulnerability\\n\\n**Do NOT open a public GitHub issue for security vulnerabilities.**\\n\\nPlease report suspected vulnerabilities privately so maintainers can triage and\\npatch them before public disclosure. Choose whichever channel you prefer:\\n\\n1. **GitHub Security Advisories (preferred).** Navigate to the\\n   *Security* tab → *Advisories* → *New draft security advisory*. This enables\\n   a private collaboration workspace and, once accepted, can be published as a\\n   GitHub-reviewed advisory (and optionally assigned a CVE).\\n2. **Email.** Send a description, reproduction steps, and any proof-of-concept\\n   to **security@fed-dup.dev** (replace with your project\'s address). Encrypt\\n   sensitive payloads with the project\'s PGP key if one is published.\\n\\n### What to include\\n\\n- Affected version (and commit hash if building from source).\\n- A concise description of the issue and its impact.\\n- Step-by-step reproduction instructions.\\n- Suggested fix or mitigation (optional but appreciated).\\n- Your preferred disclosure timeline.\\n\\n### Response timeline\\n\\n| Step                         | Target      |\\n|------------------------------|-------------|\\n| Acknowledge receipt          | ≤ 48 hours  |\\n| Initial triage & severity    | ≤ 5 days    |\\n| Fix or mitigation published  | ≤ 30 days (severity dependent) |\\n| Public advisory / CVE        | After fix is released, coordinated with reporter |\\n\\nWe follow a **coordinated disclosure** model. Reporters are credited in the\\nadvisory unless they prefer to remain anonymous.\\n\\n## Security Considerations Specific to Fed-Dup\\n\\nFed-Dup handles **Git access tokens** for both source and destination hosts.\\nReview the following before deploying.\\n\\n### Token handling\\n\\n- Tokens are injected into Git remote URLs at runtime using the\\n  `https://oauth2:{token}@host/...` form. They are **never** written to disk by\\n  the engine itself.\\n- All error messages returned by `feddup.engine.duplicate_repository` are\\n  passed through `_redact()`, which strips any token substrings before logging\\n  or surfacing to the UI.\\n- The `config.json` file stores tokens in **plaintext**. It is excluded from\\n  version control via `.gitignore`. In production, prefer environment variables\\n  (see `.env.example`) or a secrets manager, and restrict file permissions:\\n  ```bash\\n  chmod 600 config.json\\n  ```\\n\\n### URL validation\\n\\n- `feddup.utils.validate_source_url` restricts source hosts to an allow-list\\n  (`github.com`, `gitlab.com`, `bitbucket.org`, `codeberg.org`, `gitea.com`).\\n  Destination URLs are validated separately and may target any HTTPS host.\\n- `sanitize_git_url` strips embedded credentials from URLs before they are\\n  logged or displayed, and upgrades `http://` to `https://`.\\n\\n### Workspace isolation\\n\\n- Each mirror operation runs inside `./feddup_workspace/` (configurable). The\\n  workspace is cleaned up after each sync when `cleanup_after_sync` is enabled.\\n- In Docker deployments the workspace is a named volume, preventing leakage\\n  into the host filesystem.\\n\\n### Network exposure\\n\\n- The Streamlit UI binds to `0.0.0.0:8501` by default. In production, place it\\n  behind a reverse proxy with TLS termination and authentication (e.g., Caddy,\\n  Traefik, or nginx + OAuth proxy).\\n- Do **not** expose the UI directly to the public internet without an\\n  authentication layer — anyone with UI access can read configured tokens.\\n\\n### Dependency supply chain\\n\\n- The project uses Dependabot, `dependency-review-action`, and OpenSSF\\n  Scorecards workflows to monitor third-party dependencies.\\n- Lock-file hashes are verified during CI builds.\\n\\n## Hardening Checklist\\n\\n- [ ] Rotate tokens regularly (GitHub/GitLab support scoped, expiring tokens).\\n- [ ] Use fine-grained PATs with **read-only** scope for source repositories.\\n- [ ] Use separate, scoped tokens for each destination host.\\n- [ ] Enable `cleanup_after_sync` so mirrored repos do not persist on disk.\\n- [ ] Run the Docker image as a non-root user (the provided `Dockerfile` does).\\n- [ ] Mount `config.json` read-only when running in CI/cron mode (`--once`).\\n- [ ] Put the Streamlit UI behind TLS + authentication in production.\\n- [ ] Monitor logs for `REDACTED` placeholders — their presence indicates a\\n      token leaked into an error path and should be reported.\\n\\n## Contact\\n\\n- Security advisories: GitHub *Security* tab.\\n- General questions: GitHub Discussions (not for vulnerability reports).\\n"}'}}, {'id': No SQLite, no Postgres, no Redis.\\n- **Token-safe** — credentials are injected at runtime and redacted from every error message and log line.\\n- **Multi-host** — mirror from GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket, Codeberg, and Gitea to any HTTPS Git destination.\\n- **Two-phase sync** — `git clone --mirror` / `git remote update` followed by `git push --mirror` for true, ref-for-ref fidelity (branches, tags, notes, PR refs).\\n- **Web UI** — a Streamlit dashboard for adding/removing repos, editing credentials, and triggering syncs with a live progress bar.\\n- **Background worker** — a long-running `worker.py` loop or `--once` mode for cron / CI pipelines.\\n- **Docker-ready** — slim image, healthcheck against `/_stcore/health`, and a `docker-compose.yml` for one-command deploys.\\n- **CI/CD baked in** — 16 GitHub Actions workflows covering build, test, lint, release, publish, CodeQL, Scorecards, Dependabot, stale triage, and more.\\n- **Forward-compatible config** — missing keys are backfilled from defaults, so old `config.json` files keep working after upgrades.\\n\\n## 🚀 Quick Start\\n\\n### Prerequisites\\n\\n- Python **3.11+**\\n- **Git** installed and on `PATH`\\n- A source-host access token with read scope (GitHub PAT, GitLab token, etc.)\\n- A destination-host access token with write scope\\n\\n### Install\\n\\n```bash\\ngit clone https://github.com/feddup/fed-dup.git\\ncd fed-dup\\npython -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate\\npip install -r requirements.txt\\n```\\n\\n### Configure\\n\\n```bash\\ncp config.json.example config.json\\n# Edit config.json — add your tokens and repository list\\n```\\n\\n### Run the Web UI\\n\\n```bash\\nstreamlit run app.py\\n```\\n\\nThe dashboard opens at <http://localhost:8501>.\\n\\n### Run the background worker\\n\\n```bash\\n# Continuous loop (reads interval from config.json)\\npython worker.py\\n\\n# Single sync pass (ideal for cron / CI)\\npython worker.py --once\\n```\\n\\n### Docker\\n\\n```bash\\ndocker compose up -d\\n# UI at http://localhost:8501\\n```\\n\\n## 📖 How It Works\\n\\nFed-Dup performs a **true mirror** of each repository:\\n\\n1. **Clone / update** — if the repo is not yet in the local workspace, it runs `git clone --mirror <source>`. On subsequent syncs it runs `git remote update` to fetch all ref updates.\\n2. **Push** — it then runs `git push --mirror <destination>`, which forces every ref (branches, tags, notes, pull-request refs, etc.) on the destination to match the source exactly.\\n\\nToken injection uses the `https://oauth2:{token}@host/path.git` URL form, which is accepted by GitHub, GitLab, Gitea, and Codeberg. Before any URL is logged or displayed, `sanitize_git_url` strips the embedded credentials and ensures the `https://` protocol and `.git` suffix are present.\\n\\n## 🗂️ Project Structure\\n\\n```\\nfed-dup/\\n├── feddup/                # Core Python package\\n│   ├── __init__.py        #   Public API re-exports\\n│   ├── engine.py          #   duplicate_repository() — clone + push\\n│   ├── config.py          #   JSON config load/save/mutate\\n│   ├── utils.py           #   URL sanitization, validation, helpers\\n│   └── logger.py          #   Idempotent logging setup\\n├── app.py                 # Streamlit web UI\\n├── worker.py              # Background sync loop / --once mode\\n├── tests/                 # pytest suite (46 tests)\\n├── .github/workflows/     # 16 CI/CD workflows\\n├── docs/                  # Extended documentation\\n├── Dockerfile             # Slim production image\\n├── docker-compose.yml     # One-command deploy\\n└── config.json.example    # Configuration template\\n```\\n\\n## ⚙️ Configuration\\n\\n`config.json` holds everything:\\n\\n```json\\n{\\n  \\"github_token\\": \\"\\",\\n  \\"backup_token\\": \\"\\",\\n  \\"repositories\\": [\\n    {\\n      \\"source\\": \\"https://github.com/owner/repo\\",\\n      \\"destination\\": \\"https://gitea.com/backup/repo\\"\\n    }\\n  ],\\n  \\"settings\\": {\\n    \\"auto_sync_interval\\": 3600,\\n    \\"cleanup_after_sync\\": true,\\n    \\"max_parallel_syncs\\": 3\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\n| Setting                | Default | Description                                              |\\n|------------------------|---------|----------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `auto_sync_interval`   | `3600`  | Seconds between automatic sync passes (worker loop).     |\\n| `cleanup_after_sync`   | `true`  | Delete the local mirror after each push to save disk.    |\\n| `max_parallel_syncs`   | `3`     | Maximum concurrent repository syncs.                     |\\n\\nEnvironment-variable overrides are documented in [`.env.example`](.env.example).\\n\\n## 🧪 Testing\\n\\n```bash\\npip install pytest pytest-cov\\npytest --cov=feddup --cov-report=term-missing\\n```\\n\\nAll 46 tests should pass. See [`tests/`](tests/) for the full suite.\\n\\n## 🤝 Contributing\\n\\nContributions are welcome! Please read [**CONTRIBUTING.md**](CONTRIBUTING.md)\\nfor the development workflow, commit-message conventions (Conventional Commits),\\nand pull-request checklist. Loads custom `styles.css` when\\n  present.\\n- **Background worker** (`worker.py`): continuous `auto_sync_loop()` reading\\n  the interval from config, plus `--once` mode for cron / CI pipelines.\\n- **Token redaction** (`feddup/engine._redact`): all error messages and\\n  subprocess output are scrubbed of token substrings before logging or\\n  returning to the caller.\\n- **Test suite** (`tests/`): 46 pytest tests covering utils, config, and\\n  engine (with `subprocess.run` mocked via `patch`).\\n- **Deployment**: `Dockerfile` (python:3.11-slim, git + curl, healthcheck\\n  against `/_stcore/health`), `docker-compose.yml` with named volumes,\\n  `.dockerignore`, `.env.example`, `config.json.example`.\\n- **CI/CD**: 16 GitHub Actions workflows — build, test, ci, cd, deploy,\\n  release, publish, pr (Conventional Commits title validation), stale,\\n  labeler, greetings, codeql, main, pages, dependency-review, scorecards.\\n- **Project metadata**: `.github/labeler.yml`, `.github/dependabot.yml`,\\n  `.github/CODEOWNERS`, issue / PR templates, discussion welcome README.\\n- **Documentation**: README, CONTRIBUTING, CODE_OF_CONDUCT (Contributor\\n  Covenant 2.1), SECURITY, CHANGELOG, FAQ, NOTICE, SUPPORT, INSTALL, BUILD,\\n  DEPLOYMENT, ROADMAP, ADR, AUTHORS, MAINTAINERS, GOVERNANCE, PRICING,\\n  COPYING, CITATIONS, and the full `docs/` directory.\\n- **Landing page** (`index.html`) and **social image** (`social-image.png`).\\n- **Custom stylesheet** (`styles.css`) for the Streamlit UI.\\n\\n### Security\\n- `sanitize_git_url` rewritten to preserve the `https://` protocol after\\n  stripping embedded credentials. It duplicates repositories from a source host (GitHub, GitLab,\\nBitbucket, Codeberg, Gitea) to a backup destination host using true Git\\nmirroring (`git clone --mirror` + `git push --mirror`). It ships with a\\nStreamlit web UI, a background worker, and Docker support.\\n\\n### Why \\"database-free\\"?\\n\\nMany mirroring tools require a database (SQLite, Postgres, Redis) to track\\nstate. Fed-Dup stores all configuration and repository state in a single\\nhuman-readable `config.json` file. This makes it trivial to back up, inspect,\\nversion-control, and migrate. The trade-off is that it is not designed for\\nhigh-throughput, multi-tenant SaaS scenarios — it is a self-hosted backup tool.\\n\\n### Is Fed-Dup free?\\n\\nYes. Fed-Dup is licensed under the **MIT License** and is free to use, modify,\\nand distribute. See [LICENSE](LICENSE) and [PRICING.md](PRICING.md) for\\ndetails.\\n\\n### Who maintains Fed-Dup?\\n\\nFed-Dup is a community project. See [AUTHORS.md](AUTHORS.md) and\\n[MAINTAINERS.md](MAINTAINERS.md) for the current list of contributors and\\nmaintainers. Governance is described in [GOVERNANCE.md](GOVERNANCE.md).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Setup & Configuration\\n\\n### What are the prerequisites?\\n\\nPython 3.11+, Git installed and on `PATH`, and access tokens for your source\\nand destination hosts. See [INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md) for a step-by-step guide.\\n\\n### How do I get a source-host token?\\n\\n- **GitHub:** Settings → Developer settings → Personal access tokens →\\n  Fine-grained tokens. Grant **read-only** access to the repositories you want\\n  to mirror.\\n- **GitLab:** User Settings → Access Tokens. Grant `read_repository`.\\n- **Codeberg / Gitea:** User Settings → Applications → Generate token. Grant\\n  read scope.\\n\\n### How do I get a destination-host token?\\n\\nYou need **write** access on the destination. Create an empty repository on\\nthe destination host first (Fed-Dup does not create repos — it pushes to\\nexisting ones). Then generate a token with push/write scope.\\n\\n### Where does Fed-Dup store tokens?\\n\\nTokens are stored in `config.json` in plaintext. The file is excluded from\\nversion control via `.gitignore`. For production, prefer environment variables\\n(see `.env.example`) or a secrets manager, and restrict file permissions with\\n`chmod 600 config.json`. See [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) for the full hardening\\nchecklist.\\n\\n### Can I use SSH instead of HTTPS?\\n\\nFed-Dup\'s token injection uses the HTTPS `oauth2:{token}@` URL form, which\\nworks across GitHub, GitLab, Gitea, and Codeberg. SSH is not currently\\nsupported for the automated path, but you can manually configure SSH-based\\nremotes and use the worker\'s `--once` mode with pre-authenticated URLs. SSH\\nsupport is on the roadmap.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Usage\\n\\n### What does \\"true mirror\\" mean?\\n\\nA true mirror (`git push --mirror`) forces **every** ref on the destination to\\nmatch the source exactly — branches, tags, notes, pull-request refs, and any\\nother custom refs. This means:\\n\\n- **Branches deleted on the source are deleted on the destination.**\\n- Tags are overwritten if they diverge.\\n- No merge commit is created — the destination is an exact copy.\\n\\nIf you want an **additive** backup (never delete on destination), Fed-Dup is\\nnot the right tool — consider `git push --all --follow-tags` in a custom\\nscript instead.\\n\\n### Will Fed-Dup create the destination repository?\\n\\nNo. The destination repository must already exist and be empty (or already a\\nmirror). Fed-Dup will overwrite refs but will not create the repo on the\\nremote host. Create it via your hosting platform\'s UI or API first.\\n\\n### What happens if a sync fails?\\n\\n`duplicate_repository()` returns `(False, error_message)`. The error message\\nhas all token substrings redacted via `_redact()`. In the web UI, failures are\\nshown in the sync results table. In the worker, failures are logged but do not\\nabort the loop — other repos continue to sync. Failed repos are retried on the\\nnext sync pass.\\n\\n### How much disk space do I need?\\n\\nEach repository is cloned as a bare mirror into `./feddup_workspace/`. A bare\\nmirror is typically similar in size to the `.git` directory of a full clone.\\nIf `cleanup_after_sync` is enabled (default), the mirror is deleted after each\\npush, so only the largest single repo\'s worth of space is needed at any time.\\nIf cleanup is disabled, mirrors accumulate — use `get_repo_size` in the UI to\\nmonitor usage.\\n\\n### Can I run multiple repos in parallel?\\n\\n`max_parallel_syncs` in `config.json` controls the concurrency. The default is\\n3. Parallel execution is on the roadmap for the worker; the web UI\'s \\"Sync All\\nNow\\" processes sequentially with a progress bar but is being upgraded.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Docker\\n\\n### How do I run Fed-Dup in Docker?\\n\\n```bash\\ndocker compose up -d\\n```\\n\\nThe UI is available at `http://localhost:8501`. The compose file mounts\\n`config.json` and the workspace as named volumes. See\\n[DEPLOYMENT.md](DEPLOYMENT.md) for production guidance.\\n\\n### The healthcheck is failing — what do I do?\\n\\nThe healthcheck hits `/_stcore/health` on port 8501. If it fails:\\n\\n1. Ensure Streamlit started successfully (`docker compose logs feddup`).\\n2. Verify port 8501 is not already in use on the host.\\n3. Check that `config.json` is mounted and readable inside the container.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Security\\n\\n### Are my tokens safe?\\n\\nFed-Dup never writes tokens to disk beyond `config.json`. They are injected\\ninto Git remote URLs at runtime and redacted from all error messages and\\nlogs. However, `config.json` itself stores them in plaintext — secure the\\nfile with `chmod 600` and never commit it. See [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md).\\n\\n### Should I expose the Streamlit UI to the internet?\\n\\n**No — not without an authentication layer.** Anyone with UI access can read\\nconfigured tokens and trigger syncs. Put it behind a reverse proxy with TLS\\nand authentication (Caddy, Traefik, nginx + OAuth proxy, Cloudflare Access,\\netc.). See [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) and [DEPLOYMENT.md](DEPLOYMENT.md).\\n\\n### How do I report a security vulnerability?\\n\\nDo **not** open a public issue. Use GitHub Security Advisories or email the\\nsecurity address listed in [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md). We follow coordinated\\ndisclosure.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Development\\n\\n### How do I run the tests?\\n\\n```bash\\npip install pytest pytest-cov\\npytest --cov=feddup --cov-report=term-missing\\n```\\n\\nAll 46 tests should pass. See [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md).\\n\\n### How do I contribute?\\n\\nRead [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md) for the full workflow — fork, branch,\\nConventional Commits, PR template, and the quality gates (black, flake8,\\nmypy, bandit, pytest).\\n\\n### My PR title check is failing.\\n\\nThe `pr` workflow validates PR titles against Conventional Commits. Use a\\nprefix like `feat:`, `fix:`, `docs:`, `refactor:`, `test:`, `ci:`, `build:`,\\n`chore:`, or `revert:`. Example: `feat(engine): add retry logic`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Misc\\n\\n### Where is the roadmap?\\n\\nSee [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) and [docs/ROADMAP.md](docs/ROADMAP.md).\\n\\n### How do I cite Fed-Dup in a paper or project?\\n\\nSee [CITATIONS.md](CITATIONS.md).\\n\\n### I found a bug — where do I report it?\\n\\nOpen a [bug report issue](https://github.com/feddup/fed-dup/issues/new?template=bug_report.md).\\nPlease include reproduction steps and your environment details.\\n\\n### I have a feature idea — where do I share it?\\n\\nOpen a [feature request issue](https://github.com/feddup/fed-dup/issues/new?template=feature_request.md)\\nor start a [Discussion](https://github.com/feddup/fed-dup/discussions).\\n", "file_path": "FAQ.md"}'}}, {'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-805e8de2497b8d43', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# NOTICE\\n\\nThis file is part of Fed-Dup — the Federated Repository Duplication Engine.\\n\\n## Copyright\\n\\nCopyright © 2025 Fed-Dup Contributors. It walks through configuring repositories, running\\nthe web UI, running the background worker, and understanding sync behavior.\\n\\n## Table of Contents\\n\\n- [Configuration File](#configuration-file)\\n- [Adding Repositories via the Web UI](#adding-repositories-via-the-web-ui)\\n- [Adding Repositories via config.json](#adding-repositories-via-configjson)\\n- [Running the Web UI](#running-the-web-ui)\\n- [Running the Background Worker](#running-the-background-worker)\\n- [Cron / CI Mode](#cron--ci-mode)\\n- [Docker](#docker)\\n- [Understanding the Sync Process](#understanding-the-sync-process)\\n- [Sync Results and Error Messages](#sync-results-and-error-messages)\\n- [Monitoring Disk Usage](#monitoring-disk-usage)\\n- [Environment Variables](#environment-variables)\\n- [Common Workflows](#common-workflows)\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Configuration File\\n\\nAll of Fed-Dup\'s state lives in `config.json`. Start from the example:\\n\\n```bash\\ncp config.json.example config.json\\n```\\n\\nThe structure:\\n\\n```json\\n{\\n  \\"github_token\\": \\"ghp_your_source_token\\",\\n  \\"backup_token\\": \\"your_destination_token\\",\\n  \\"repositories\\": [\\n    {\\n      \\"source\\": \\"https://github.com/owner/repo\\",\\n      \\"destination\\": \\"https://gitea.com/backup/repo\\"\\n    }\\n  ],\\n  \\"settings\\": {\\n    \\"auto_sync_interval\\": 3600,\\n    \\"cleanup_after_sync\\": true,\\n    \\"max_parallel_syncs\\": 3\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\n| Field                  | Type    | Description                                                  |\\n|------------------------|---------|--------------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `github_token`         | string  | Token for the **source** host (read access).                 |\\n| `backup_token`         | string  | Token for the **destination** host (write access).           |\\n| `repositories`         | array   | List of `{source, destination}` objects.                     |\\n| `repositories[].source`      | string | Full HTTPS URL of the source repo (no token needed in URL). |\\n| `repositories[].destination` | string | Full HTTPS URL of the destination repo.                     |\\n| `settings.auto_sync_interval`   | int  | Seconds between syncs in the worker loop. Default: `3600`. |\\n| `settings.cleanup_after_sync`   | bool | Delete local mirror after push. Default: `true`.           |\\n| `settings.max_parallel_syncs`   | int  | Max concurrent syncs. Default: `3`.                         |\\n\\n> **Note:** `github_token` is used as the source token regardless of the\\n> actual source host (GitHub, GitLab, etc.). Similarly, `backup_token` is the\\n> destination token. Both are injected at runtime and never stored in the\\n> repository URLs themselves.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Adding Repositories via the Web UI\\n\\n1. Launch the UI:\\n   ```bash\\n   streamlit run app.py\\n   ```\\n2. In the **sidebar**, enter your source and destination tokens under\\n   *Credentials*.\\n3. Under *Sync Settings*, adjust the auto-sync interval, cleanup toggle, and\\n   max parallel syncs as desired. Click **Save Settings**.\\n4. In the main panel under *Repository Management*, enter the **Source URL**\\n   and **Destination URL** and click **Add Repository**.\\n5. The repository appears in the list. Click **Sync** next to any repo for a\\n   one-off mirror, or use **Sync All Now** for a full pass with a progress\\n   bar.\\n\\nThe UI validates source URLs against the host allow-list and sanitizes all\\nURLs before display, so tokens never appear on screen.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Adding Repositories via config.json\\n\\nYou can also edit `config.json` directly — useful for bulk imports or CI\\nprovisioning:\\n\\n```json\\n{\\n  \\"github_token\\": \\"ghp_abc123\\",\\n  \\"backup_token\\": \\"def456\\",\\n  \\"repositories\\": [\\n    {\\"source\\": \\"https://github.com/org/repo-a\\", \\"destination\\": \\"https://gitea.com/backup/repo-a\\"},\\n    {\\"source\\": \\"https://github.com/org/repo-b\\", \\"destination\\": \\"https://gitea.com/backup/repo-b\\"},\\n    {\\"source\\": \\"https://gitlab.com/org/repo-c\\", \\"destination\\": \\"https://codeberg.org/backup/repo-c\\"}\\n  ],\\n  \\"settings\\": {\\n    \\"auto_sync_interval\\": 1800,\\n    \\"cleanup_after_sync\\": true,\\n    \\"max_parallel_syncs\\": 5\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\nFed-Dup backfills any missing `settings` keys from defaults, so you can omit\\nthe `settings` block entirely if you want the defaults.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Running the Web UI\\n\\n```bash\\nstreamlit run app.py\\n```\\n\\nThe dashboard is available at <http://localhost:8501>.\\n\\nKey sections:\\n\\n- **Sidebar → Credentials:** source and destination tokens. Saved to\\n  `config.json`.\\n- **Sidebar → Sync Settings:** interval, cleanup, parallelism. Saved to\\n  `config.json`.\\n- **Repository Management:** add, remove, and individually sync repos.\\n- **Sync All Now:** runs a full sync pass with a live progress bar and\\n  per-repo results table.\\n- **Dashboard:** summary metrics — total repos, last sync time, success/failure\\n  counts.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Running the Background Worker\\n\\nThe worker runs a continuous loop, syncing all configured repositories at the\\ninterval specified in `config.json`:\\n\\n```bash\\npython worker.py\\n```\\n\\nOutput is logged to the console (and any configured log handler). The worker\\nreads `settings.auto_sync_interval` on each pass, so changing it in\\n`config.json` (or via the UI) takes effect on the next iteration without a\\nrestart.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Cron / CI Mode\\n\\nFor scheduled or pipeline-driven syncs, use `--once` to run a single pass and\\nexit:\\n\\n```bash\\npython worker.py --once\\n```\\n\\nThis is ideal for:\\n\\n- **Cron jobs:**\\n  ```cron\\n  0 */6 * * *  cd /opt/fed-dup && /opt/fed-dup/.venv/bin/python worker.py --once >> /var/log/fed-dup.log 2>&1\\n  ```\\n- **GitHub Actions / GitLab CI:** add a scheduled workflow that runs\\n  `worker.py --once` against a config populated from repository secrets.\\n- **Kubernetes CronJobs:** container image runs `worker.py --once` on a\\n  schedule.\\n\\nWith `--once`, the process exits with code `0` on success or non-zero if any\\nrepo failed to sync, making it suitable for CI gating.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Docker\\n\\n```bash\\n# Build and start\\ndocker compose up -d\\n\\n# View logs\\ndocker compose logs -f feddup\\n\\n# Stop\\ndocker compose down\\n```\\n\\nThe compose file:\\n- Exposes port **8501** for the Streamlit UI.\\n- Mounts `config.json` as a volume so changes persist.\\n- Mounts `feddup_workspace` as a named volume for mirror storage.\\n- Runs a healthcheck against `/_stcore/health`.\\n\\nFor production hardening, see [DEPLOYMENT.md](DEPLOYMENT.md).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Understanding the Sync Process\\n\\nFor each repository, `duplicate_repository()` performs two phases:\\n\\n### Phase 1 — Clone / Update\\n\\n- If the repo does not yet exist in `./feddup_workspace/`, run:\\n  ```\\n  git clone --mirror https://oauth2:{source_token}@github.com/owner/repo.git\\n  ```\\n- If it already exists, run:\\n  ```\\n  git remote update\\n  ```\\n  This fetches all ref updates from the source.\\n\\n### Phase 2 — Push\\n\\n```\\ngit push --mirror https://oauth2:{dest_token}@gitea.com/backup/repo.git\\n```\\n\\nThis forces **every** ref on the destination to match the source — branches,\\ntags, notes, and PR refs. Branches deleted on the source are deleted on the\\ndestination.\\n\\n### Cleanup\\n\\nIf `cleanup_after_sync` is enabled, the local mirror directory is deleted\\nafter a successful push, freeing disk space.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Sync Results and Error Messages\\n\\n`duplicate_repository()` returns a tuple `(success: bool, message: str)`.\\n\\n- **On success:** `(True, \\"Repository mirrored successfully\\")`\\n- **On failure:** `(False, \\"<error details>\\")` — all token substrings are\\n  redacted by `_redact()` before the message is returned or logged.\\n\\nIn the web UI, results are shown in a table. In the worker, results are\\nlogged. Common failures:\\n\\n| Error (redacted form)              | Likely Cause                              |\\n|------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------|\\n| `fatal: Authentication failed`     | Wrong or expired token.                   |\\n| `fatal: could not read Username`   | Empty token.                              |\\n| `fatal: repository not found`      | Wrong URL, or token lacks access.         |\\n| `fatal: remote rejected`           | Destination repo missing or not empty.    |\\n| `error: failed to push some refs`  | Non-fast-forward on destination; ensure it is a true mirror. |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Monitoring Disk Usage\\n\\nThe UI displays each repo\'s size via `get_repo_size` / `humanize_size`. If\\n`cleanup_after_sync` is disabled, mirrors accumulate in\\n`./feddup_workspace/`. Monitor with:\\n\\n```bash\\ndu -sh feddup_workspace/\\n```\\n\\nTo reclaim space, enable `cleanup_after_sync` or manually remove workspace\\ndirectories (they are safe to delete — they are just bare mirrors).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Environment Variables\\n\\nFed-Dup reads tokens from `config.json` first. You can also use environment\\nvariables (see `.env.example`) for 12-factor / container deployments:\\n\\n| Variable          | Purpose                          |\\n|-------------------|----------------------------------|\\n| `FEDDUP_GITHUB_TOKEN`  | Source host token.          |\\n| `FEDDUP_BACKUP_TOKEN`  | Destination host token.     |\\n| `FEDDUP_CONFIG_PATH`   | Path to config.json (default: `./config.json`). |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Common Workflows\\n\\n### Mirror all repos from a GitHub org to Gitea\\n\\n1. Create empty repos on Gitea for each source repo.\\n2. Add each `{source, destination}` pair to `config.json`.\\n3. Run `python worker.py --once` for an initial mirror.\\n4. Set up a cron job or scheduled CI for ongoing syncs.\\n\\n### One-off mirror from the UI\\n\\n1. Add the repo in *Repository Management*.\\n2. Click **Sync**.\\n\\n### Debug a failing sync\\n\\n1. Run `python worker.py --once` and read the redacted error message.\\n2. Verify the source URL is on the allow-list (github.com, gitlab.com,\\n   bitbucket.org, codeberg.org, gitea.com).\\n3. Verify the destination repo exists and is empty.\\n4. Verify token scopes (read for source, write for destination).\\n5. Check [FAQ.md](FAQ.md) and [SUPPORT.md](SUPPORT.md) for more help.\\n", "file_path": "usage.md"}'}}, {'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-abd9d0f108a80d03', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', Ensure a clean environment\\n\\n```bash\\ncd fed-dup\\npython -m venv .venv\\nsource .venv/bin/activate\\npip install --upgrade pip build wheel\\npip install -r requirements.txt\\n```\\n\\n### 2. Run quality gates\\n\\nBefore building, ensure the code passes all checks:\\n\\n```bash\\nblack --check . && flake8 . && mypy feddup/ && pytest\\n```\\n\\nSee [Running Quality Gates](#running-quality-gates) below.\\n\\n### 3. Build the distributions\\n\\n```bash\\npython -m build\\n```\\n\\nThis produces:\\n\\n```\\ndist/\\n├── feddup-1.0.0-py3-none-any.whl   # Wheel\\n└── feddup-1.0.0.tar.gz             # Source distribution\\n```\\n\\n### 4. However, if you want to generate a static site (e.g., for\\nGitHub Pages):\\n\\n### GitHub Pages\\n\\nThe `.github/workflows/pages.yml` workflow automatically builds and deploys\\nthe docs site from the `docs/` directory on pushes to `main`. For\\n  fully reproducible builds, generate a lock file:\\n  ```bash\\n  pip freeze > requirements.lock\\n  ```\\n  and install with `pip install -r requirements.lock`.\\n- **Pin CI actions:** All GitHub Actions in the workflows use pinned versions\\n  (and where possible, SHA-pinned for security via Scorecards).\\n- **Use the same base image:** The Dockerfile pins `python:3.11-slim`. Prepare configuration\\n\\n```bash\\ncp config.json.example config.json\\n# Edit config.json with your tokens and repositories\\nchmod 600 config.json\\n```\\n\\n### 2. Start the service\\n\\n```bash\\ndocker compose up -d\\n```\\n\\n### 3. Verify health\\n\\n```bash\\ndocker compose ps\\n# The STATUS column should show \\"Up (healthy)\\"\\n```\\n\\n### 4. View logs\\n\\n```bash\\ndocker compose logs -f feddup\\n```\\n\\nThe compose file:\\n- Maps port **8501** → host **8501**.\\n- Mounts `./config.json` into the container (read-write for UI edits).\\n- Creates a named volume `feddup_workspace` for mirror storage.\\n- Runs a healthcheck against `/_stcore/health`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Standalone Docker\\n\\n```bash\\ndocker build -t feddup:1.0.0 .\\n\\ndocker run -d \\\\\\n  --name feddup \\\\\\n  -p 8501:8501 \\\\\\n  -v $(pwd)/config.json:/app/config.json \\\\\\n  -v feddup_workspace:/app/feddup_workspace \\\\\\n  --restart unless-stopped \\\\\\n  feddup:1.0.0\\n```\\n\\nCheck health:\\n```bash\\ncurl http://localhost:8501/_stcore/health\\n# Expected: \\"ok\\"\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Kubernetes\\n\\nA minimal Kubernetes deployment:\\n\\n### ConfigMap (non-secret settings)\\n\\n```yaml\\napiVersion: v1\\nkind: ConfigMap\\nmetadata:\\n  name: feddup-config\\ndata:\\n  auto_sync_interval: \\"3600\\"\\n  cleanup_after_sync: \\"true\\"\\n  max_parallel_syncs: \\"3\\"\\n```\\n\\n### Secret (tokens)\\n\\n```yaml\\napiVersion: v1\\nkind: Secret\\nmetadata:\\n  name: feddup-secrets\\ntype: Opaque\\nstringData:\\n  github_token: \\"ghp_your_source_token\\"\\n  backup_token: \\"your_destination_token\\"\\n```\\n\\n### Deployment\\n\\n```yaml\\napiVersion: apps/v1\\nkind: Deployment\\nmetadata:\\n  name: feddup\\nspec:\\n  replicas: 1\\n  selector:\\n    matchLabels:\\n      app: feddup\\n  template:\\n    metadata:\\n      labels:\\n        app: feddup\\n    spec:\\n      containers:\\n        - name: feddup\\n          image: feddup:1.0.0\\n          ports:\\n            - containerPort: 8501\\n          envFrom:\\n            - secretRef:\\n                name: feddup-secrets\\n            - configMapRef:\\n                name: feddup-config\\n          volumeMounts:\\n            - name: workspace\\n              mountPath: /app/feddup_workspace\\n          livenessProbe:\\n            httpGet:\\n              path: /_stcore/health\\n              port: 8501\\n            initialDelaySeconds: 30\\n            periodSeconds: 30\\n          readinessProbe:\\n            httpGet:\\n              path: /_stcore/health\\n              port: 8501\\n            initialDelaySeconds: 10\\n            periodSeconds: 10\\n      volumes:\\n        - name: workspace\\n          persistentVolumeClaim:\\n            claimName: feddup-workspace\\n```\\n\\n### PVC\\n\\n```yaml\\napiVersion: v1\\nkind: PersistentVolumeClaim\\nmetadata:\\n  name: feddup-workspace\\nspec:\\n  accessModes:\\n    - ReadWriteOnce\\n  resources:\\n    requests:\\n      storage: 10Gi\\n```\\n\\n### CronJob (CI mode)\\n\\nFor scheduled syncs without a persistent UI:\\n\\n```yaml\\napiVersion: batch/v1\\nkind: CronJob\\nmetadata:\\n  name: feddup-sync\\nspec:\\n  schedule: \\"0 */6 * * *\\"\\n  jobTemplate:\\n    spec:\\n      template:\\n        spec:\\n          containers:\\n            - name: feddup\\n              image: feddup:1.0.0\\n              command: [\\"python\\", \\"worker.py\\", \\"--once\\"]\\n              envFrom:\\n                - secretRef:\\n                    name: feddup-secrets\\n          restartPolicy: OnFailure\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Systemd Service\\n\\nFor bare-metal or VM deployments without Docker:\\n\\n### 1. Install Fed-Dup\\n\\nFollow [INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md).\\n\\n### 2. Create a systemd unit\\n\\n`/etc/systemd/system/feddup.service`:\\n\\n```ini\\n[Unit]\\nDescription=Fed-Dup Repository Mirroring Engine\\nAfter=network.target\\n\\n[Service]\\nType=simple\\nUser=feddup\\nGroup=feddup\\nWorkingDirectory=/opt/fed-dup\\nEnvironmentFile=/opt/fed-dup/.env\\nExecStart=/opt/fed-dup/.venv/bin/streamlit run app.py --server.port=8501 --server.address=127.0.0.1\\nRestart=on-failure\\nRestartSec=10\\n\\n[Install]\\nWantedBy=multi-user.target\\n```\\n\\n### 3. Create the user and set permissions\\n\\n```bash\\nsudo useradd -r -s /bin/false -d /opt/fed-dup feddup\\nsudo chown -R feddup:feddup /opt/fed-dup\\nsudo chmod 600 /opt/fed-dup/config.json\\n```\\n\\n### 4. Enable and start\\n\\n```bash\\nsudo systemctl daemon-reload\\nsudo systemctl enable --now feddup\\nsudo systemctl status feddup\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Cron / CI Deployment\\n\\nFor stateless, scheduled syncs using `--once`:\\n\\n```cron\\n# Sync every 6 hours\\n0 */6 * * *  cd /opt/fed-dup && /opt/fed-dup/.venv/bin/python worker.py --once >> /var/log/fed-dup.log 2>&1\\n```\\n\\nThis is ideal when you don\'t need the web UI and just want automated\\nmirroring.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Reverse Proxy & TLS\\n\\n**Do not expose the Streamlit UI directly to the internet.** Use a reverse\\nproxy with TLS termination.\\n\\n### Caddy (automatic TLS)\\n\\n```caddyfile\\nfeddup.example.com {\\n    reverse_proxy localhost:8501\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### nginx\\n\\n```nginx\\nserver {\\n    listen 443 ssl http2;\\n    server_name feddup.example.com;\\n\\n    ssl_certificate     /etc/ssl/certs/feddup.crt;\\n    ssl_certificate_key /etc/ssl/private/feddup.key;\\n\\n    location / {\\n        proxy_pass http://127.0.0.1:8501;\\n        proxy_http_version 1.1;\\n        proxy_set_header Upgrade $http_upgrade;\\n        proxy_set_header Connection \\"upgrade\\";\\n        proxy_set_header Host $host;\\n        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;\\n        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;\\n        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;\\n        proxy_read_timeout 86400;\\n    }\\n}\\n\\nserver {\\n    listen 80;\\n    server_name feddup.example.com;\\n    return 301 https://$host$request_uri;\\n}\\n```\\n\\n> **WebSocket support is required** — Streamlit uses WebSockets for live\\n> updates. The `Upgrade` and `Connection` headers above enable this.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Authentication\\n\\nStreamlit does not include built-in authentication. Add an auth layer at the\\nreverse proxy:\\n\\n- **OAuth Proxy:** Use [oauth2-proxy](https://oauth2-proxy.github.io/) with\\n  GitHub, Google, or OIDC.\\n- **Cloudflare Access:** Zero-trust access in front of the proxy.\\n- **Basic Auth (nginx):** For small teams:\\n  ```nginx\\n  auth_basic \\"Fed-Dup\\";\\n  auth_basic_user_file /etc/nginx/.htpasswd;\\n  ```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Persistent Storage\\n\\n| Data              | Location                  | Must Persist? |\\n|-------------------|---------------------------|---------------|\\n| `config.json`     | Mounted volume / file     | ✅ Yes — all settings & tokens |\\n| `feddup_workspace/` | Named volume / PVC      | Optional — if `cleanup_after_sync` is true, this is just temp space. If false, mirrors accumulate here. |\\n\\nFor Kubernetes, use a `PersistentVolumeClaim`. For Docker Compose, the named\\nvolume handles persistence automatically.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Health Checks & Monitoring\\n\\n### Health endpoint\\n\\nStreamlit exposes `/_stcore/health` which returns `ok` (HTTP 200) when the\\nserver is up.\\n\\n```bash\\ncurl http://localhost:8501/_stcore/health\\n```\\n\\n### Docker healthcheck\\n\\nThe `Dockerfile` includes:\\n```dockerfile\\nHEALTHCHECK CMD curl --fail http://localhost:8501/_stcore/health || exit 1\\n```\\n\\n### External monitoring\\n\\nPoint your monitoring tool (Uptime Kuma, Pingdom, Prometheus blackbox\\nexporter) at the health endpoint.\\n\\n### Logging\\n\\n- The worker logs to stdout. In Docker, view with `docker compose logs`.\\n- In systemd, logs go to journald: `journalctl -u feddup -f`.\\n- All token substrings are redacted before logging.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Security Hardening\\n\\nSee [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) for the full checklist. Key production items:\\n\\n1. ✅ **TLS** — terminate TLS at the reverse proxy.\\n2. ✅ **Authentication** — add an auth layer (oauth2-proxy, Cloudflare Access,\\n   basic auth).\\n3. ✅ **File permissions** — `chmod 600 config.json`; run as non-root user.\\n4. ✅ **Token scope** — use read-only tokens for sources, scoped write tokens\\n   for destinations.\\n5. ✅ **Token rotation** — rotate regularly; use expiring tokens where\\n   supported.\\n6. ✅ **Network isolation** — restrict egress to known Git hosts only if\\n   possible.\\n7. ✅ **Resource limits** — set CPU/memory limits in Docker/K8s to prevent\\n   runaway syncs.\\n8. ✅ **Cleanup** — enable `cleanup_after_sync` to minimize disk usage.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Backup & Recovery\\n\\nFed-Dup itself is a backup tool, but it also needs to be backed up:\\n\\n1. **Back up `config.json`** — this is the only stateful file. Store it in a\\n   secrets manager or encrypted backup. It contains tokens, so treat it as a\\n   secret.\\n2. **Back up the destination repos** — your mirrored repos *are* the backup.\\n   Verify they are accessible and complete periodically.\\n3. **Recovery:** To restore, redeploy Fed-Dup and restore `config.json`. The\\n   workspace directory is ephemeral (it is just bare mirrors) and does not\\n   need to be backed up if `cleanup_after_sync` is enabled.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Production Checklist\\n\\n- [ ] TLS enabled at reverse proxy\\n- [ ] Authentication layer configured\\n- [ ] `config.json` permissions set to `600`\\n- [ ] Running as non-root user\\n- [ ] Token scopes minimized (read-only source, scoped write dest)\\n- [ ] `cleanup_after_sync` enabled (if disk space is a concern)\\n- [ ] Health check monitored externally\\n- [ ] Logs being collected and reviewed\\n- [ ] Resource limits set (Docker/K8s)\\n- [ ] `config.json` backed up to a secure location\\n- [ ] Destination repos verified as accessible\\n", "file_path": "DEPLOYMENT.md"}'}}, {'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-a7885d705c7daf42', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# Roadmap\\n\\nThis document outlines the planned direction for Fed-Dup. It is a living\\ndocument — items may be reprioritized, and community input is welcome via\\n[GitHub Discussions](https://github.com/feddup/fed-dup/discussions) and\\nfeature requests.\\n\\n## How to Influence the Roadmap\\n\\n- **Upvote** existing feature requests with 👍.\\n- **Open** a new feature request describing your use case.\\n- **Sponsor** a feature via [Ko-fi](https://ko-fi.com/feddup) to accelerate\\n  development.\\n- **Contribute** a PR — see [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Current Status\\n\\n**v1.0.0** is the initial stable release. It provides:\\n\\n- Two-phase true Git mirroring (`clone --mirror` + `push --mirror`).\\n- Streamlit web UI with progress tracking.\\n- Background worker with `--once` cron/CI mode.\\n- JSON-based, database-free configuration.\\n- Token redaction and URL sanitization.\\n- Docker and Docker Compose deployment.\\n- Full CI/CD with 16 GitHub Actions workflows.\\n- Comprehensive documentation and test suite (46 tests).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Short-Term (v1.1)\\n\\nFocus: **Performance & observability.**\\n\\n- [ ] **Parallel sync execution** — a real concurrency pool honoring\\n      `max_parallel_syncs`, replacing the current sequential loop in the\\n      worker and UI \\"Sync All Now\\".\\n- [ ] **Sync status history** — persist per-repo last-sync time, duration,\\n      and result (success/failure) in `config.json` for dashboard display.\\n- [ ] **Failure retry with exponential backoff** — automatically retry\\n      transient failures before reporting them as failed.\\n- [ ] **Structured logging** — optional JSON log output for log aggregation\\n      systems (ELK, Loki, Datadog).\\n- [ ] **Per-repository settings override** — allow individual repos to\\n      override global settings (e.g., cleanup, schedule).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Medium-Term (v1.2 – v1.5)\\n\\nFocus: **Automation & extensibility.**\\n\\n- [ ] **Webhook-triggered sync** — expose a webhook endpoint that triggers an\\n      immediate sync for a specific repo or all repos when a push event is\\n      received from the source host.\\n- [ ] **Per-repository cron scheduling** — each repo can have its own sync\\n      schedule via cron expressions, independent of the global interval.\\n- [ ] **SSH transport support** — allow SSH-based Git remotes (with deploy\\n      keys) as an alternative to HTTPS token injection.\\n- [ ] **Web UI authentication** — built-in password or OIDC login so the UI\\n      can be safely exposed without an external auth proxy.\\n- [ ] **Multi-destination fan-out** — mirror one source to multiple\\n      destinations simultaneously.\\n- [ ] **Plugin / hook system** — run pre-sync and post-sync hooks (shell\\n      scripts or Python callables) for custom logic (notifications, size\\n      checks, etc.).\\n- [ ] **Mirror verification** — post-push verification step that compares ref\\n      counts between source and destination to confirm the mirror is exact.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Long-Term (v2.0+)\\n\\nFocus: **Scale & alternative backends.**\\n\\n- [ ] **Optional database backend** — for users who want multi-tenant,\\n      high-throughput operation, add an optional SQLite/Postgres backend\\n      (JSON config remains the default for simple deployments).\\n- [ ] **Object storage destination** — push mirror bundles to S3, GCS, Azure\\n      Blob, or Backblaze B2 as an alternative to a live Git host.\\n- [ ] **Differential / incremental sync** — reduce bandwidth by detecting\\n      when no refs have changed and skipping the push phase.\\n- [ ] **Distributed worker pool** — for very large repo counts, support\\n      multiple worker nodes coordinating via a shared backend.\\n- [ ] **GraphQL / REST API** — a programmatic API alongside the Streamlit UI\\n      for integration with other tools and automation platforms.\\n- [ ] **Built-in notifications** — email, Slack, Discord, or webhook\\n      notifications on sync success/failure.\\n- [ ] **Mirror health dashboard** — a Grafana-compatible metrics endpoint\\n      (Prometheus) for monitoring mirror freshness and failure rates.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Completed Milestones\\n\\n### v1.0.0 — 2025-09-01 ✅\\n\\n- Initial stable release.\\n- Core engine, config, utils, logger.\\n- Streamlit web UI and background worker.\\n- 46-test pytest suite.\\n- Docker + Docker Compose.\\n- 16 GitHub Actions workflows.\\n- Full documentation suite.\\n\\nSee [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md) for details.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Deprecation Policy\\n\\n- Features are deprecated with a notice in [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md) and\\n  the `CHANGELOG` `[Deprecated]` section.\\n- Deprecated features receive bug fixes for at least **one minor release\\n  cycle** before removal.\\n- Breaking changes require a **major version bump** (e.g., v2.0.0) and are\\n  documented with a migration guide.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Versioning\\n\\nFed-Dup follows [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/):\\n\\n| Bump   | When                                        |\\n|--------|---------------------------------------------|\\n| PATCH  | Bug fixes, no new features                  |\\n| MINOR  | New features, backward-compatible           |\\n| MAJOR  | Breaking changes (requires migration guide) |\\n\\n---\\n\\n*This roadmap is aspirational and not a commitment. Dates and priorities are\\nsubject to change based on community feedback and maintainer availability.*\\n", "file_path": "ROADMAP.md"}'}}, {'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-b6f9a9f45acc56b4', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# Architecture Decision Records (ADR)\\n\\nThis document collects the key architectural decisions made during the\\ndesign and development of Fed-Dup. Each record describes a decision, its\\ncontext, the chosen alternative, and the consequences.\\n\\nThe format is inspired by [Michael Nygard\'s ADR template](http://thinkrelevance.com/blog/2011/11/15/documenting-architecture-decisions).\\n\\n## Table of Contents\\n\\n- [ADR-0001 — Database-free Configuration](#adr-0001--database-free-configuration)\\n- [ADR-0002 — True Git Mirroring](#adr-0002--true-git-mirroring)\\n- [ADR-0003 — HTTPS Token Injection](#adr-0003--https-token-injection)\\n- [ADR-0004 — Streamlit for the Web UI](#adr-0004--streamlit-for-the-web-ui)\\n- [ADR-0005 — Background Worker with --once Mode](#adr-0005--background-worker-with---once-mode)\\n- [ADR-0006 — Token Redaction in Error Paths](#adr-0006--token-redaction-in-error-paths)\\n- [ADR-0007 — Forward-compatible Config Backfilling](#adr-0007--forward-compatible-config-backfilling)\\n- [ADR-0008 — Host Allow-list for Source URLs](#adr-0008--host-allow-list-for-source-urls)\\n- [ADR-0009 — Docker as the Primary Deployment Vehicle](#adr-0009--docker-as-the-primary-deployment-vehicle)\\n- [ADR-0010 — MIT License](#adr-0010--mit-license)\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0001 — Database-free Configuration\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nFed-Dup needs to persist configuration (tokens, repository list, settings)\\nand optionally sync state. Many mirroring tools use SQLite, Postgres, or Redis\\nfor this. However, the target users are individuals and small teams who want\\na simple, self-hosted backup tool with minimal operational overhead.\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\nStore all configuration in a single `config.json` file. No database.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Trivial to install — no database to provision or manage.\\n- Human-readable and editable.\\n- Easy to back up (copy one file).\\n- Easy to version-control (tokens redacted / gitignored).\\n- Portable across environments.\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- Not suitable for high-throughput, multi-tenant, or concurrent-write\\n  scenarios (no transactional updates).\\n- Sync state history is limited (mitigated in v1.1 by storing last-sync info\\n  in the JSON file).\\n\\n**Mitigation:** For users who need a database backend, a future v2.0 may add\\nan optional SQLite/Postgres backend (see [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md)).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0002 — True Git Mirroring\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nFed-Dup must duplicate repositories to a backup host. Options:\\n1. `git clone` + `git push` (non-mirror) — only pushes branches, loses tags,\\n   notes, and PR refs; may create merge conflicts.\\n2. `git clone --mirror` + `git push --mirror` — forces every ref to match\\n   exactly, including tags, notes, and custom refs.\\n3. Bundle-based backup — creates a `git bundle` file (not a live repo).\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\nUse option 2: `git clone --mirror` / `git remote update` followed by\\n`git push --mirror`.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Exact ref-for-ref fidelity — the destination is a true mirror.\\n- Handles all ref types (branches, tags, notes, PR refs).\\n- No merge conflicts — the destination is overwritten to match the source.\\n- Simple and well-understood Git semantics.\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- **Destructive** — branches deleted on the source are deleted on the\\n  destination. This is by design for a true mirror but may surprise users\\n  expecting additive backup.\\n- Requires the destination repo to be empty or already a mirror.\\n\\n**Mitigation:** Document this behavior clearly in [README.md](README.md) and\\n[FAQ.md](FAQ.md).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0003 — HTTPS Token Injection\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nGit needs credentials to access private repos and push to destinations.\\nOptions:\\n1. SSH keys — robust but requires per-host key management and SSH agent\\n   forwarding; not all hosts support it uniformly.\\n2. Git credential helper — stores tokens in the OS credential store; harder\\n   to configure in containers.\\n3. HTTPS URL with embedded token (`https://oauth2:{token}@host/path`) —\\n   simple, works across GitHub, GitLab, Gitea, Codeberg.\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\nUse option 3: inject the token into the HTTPS remote URL at runtime.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Works across all supported hosts (GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket, Codeberg,\\n  Gitea).\\n- No SSH key management or credential helper configuration needed.\\n- Works in containers and CI without extra setup.\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- Tokens appear in the URL — risk of leaking into logs or error messages.\\n- Tokens are passed via command-line arguments (visible in process list).\\n\\n**Mitigation:**\\n- `sanitize_git_url` strips credentials before logging or display.\\n- `_redact()` scrubs token substrings from all error messages and subprocess\\n  output.\\n- Tokens are never persisted in URLs in `config.json` — they are stored in\\n  separate fields and injected at runtime.\\n- See [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) for the full hardening checklist.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0004 — Streamlit for the Web UI\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nFed-Dup benefits from a web UI for managing repos and triggering syncs.\\nOptions:\\n1. Flask / FastAPI + custom HTML/JS — flexible but more code to maintain.\\n2. Streamlit — Python-native, rapid prototyping, built-in widgets.\\n3. No UI — CLI only.\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\nUse Streamlit.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Rapid development — the entire UI is in a single `app.py`.\\n- Python-native — no separate frontend build step.\\n- Built-in widgets (forms, buttons, progress bars, tables).\\n- Active ecosystem and good documentation.\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- Limited customization of layout and styling (mitigated by `styles.css`\\n  injection).\\n- No built-in authentication (mitigated by reverse proxy auth — see\\n  [DEPLOYMENT.md](DEPLOYMENT.md)).\\n- WebSocket-based — requires a reverse proxy that supports upgrades.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0005 — Background Worker with --once Mode\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nFed-Dup needs to sync repos automatically. Options:\\n1. Long-running loop only — requires a always-on process.\\n2. `--once` mode only — requires external scheduling (cron/CI).\\n3. Both — a loop for always-on deployments and `--once` for scheduled/CI.\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\nSupport both modes in `worker.py`.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Flexibility — always-on for self-hosted, `--once` for cron/CI/K8s CronJobs.\\n- `--once` exits with a non-zero code on failure, enabling CI gating.\\n- The loop reads the interval from config, so changes take effect without a\\n  restart.\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- Two code paths to maintain (mitigated by sharing `sync_all()` between them).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0006 — Token Redaction in Error Paths\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nGit error messages may echo the remote URL, which contains the injected token.\\nIf these messages are logged or displayed, tokens leak.\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\nImplement `_redact(message, *tokens)` in `feddup/engine.py` and pass all\\nerror messages and subprocess output through it before logging or returning.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Tokens never appear in logs, error messages, or the UI.\\n- Defense in depth — even if a token somehow reaches an error path, it is\\n  scrubbed.\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- Slight performance overhead (string replacement) — negligible for this use\\n  case.\\n- Redacted messages may be less readable (tokens replaced with `REDACTED`),\\n  but this is an acceptable trade-off.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0007 — Forward-compatible Config Backfilling\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nAs Fed-Dup evolves, new settings will be added to `config.json`. Users with\\nold config files should not break on upgrade.\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\n`load_config()` backfills missing keys from `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` at load time.\\nThe config file is not modified unless `save_config()` is explicitly called.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Old config files keep working after upgrades.\\n- No manual migration steps required for new settings.\\n- Defaults are centralized in one place (`_default_config()`).\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- The in-memory config may differ from the on-disk file (missing keys are\\n  added in memory but not written back until `save_config()`). This is\\n  acceptable — any UI \\"Save\\" action persists the full config.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0008 — Host Allow-list for Source URLs\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nTo prevent SSRF and abuse, source URLs should be restricted to known,\\ntrustworthy Git hosts.\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\n`validate_source_url` checks the host against an allow-list:\\n`github.com`, `gitlab.com`, `bitbucket.org`, `codeberg.org`, `gitea.com`.\\nDestination URLs are validated separately (HTTPS only, any host) since the\\ndestination is user-controlled.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Reduces SSRF risk for source fetches.\\n- Clear error messages for unsupported hosts.\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- Users cannot mirror from arbitrary hosts (e.g., self-hosted Gitea on a\\n  custom domain). This is a deliberate trade-off for security.\\n\\n**Mitigation:** Users can fork the project and extend the allow-list, or a\\nfuture version may make it configurable.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0009 — Docker as the Primary Deployment Vehicle\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nFed-Dup should be easy to deploy in production. Options:\\n1. Python venv + systemd — traditional but requires per-host setup.\\n2. Docker / Docker Compose — reproducible, portable, easy to manage.\\n3. Both.\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\nProvide both, but make Docker the primary recommended path.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Docker provides a reproducible, isolated environment.\\n- `docker-compose.yml` enables one-command deploys.\\n- Healthcheck and volume management are built in.\\n- Kubernetes deployment is straightforward from the Docker image.\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- Users without Docker must use the venv + systemd path (documented in\\n  [DEPLOYMENT.md](DEPLOYMENT.md)).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ADR-0010 — MIT License\\n\\n**Date:** 2025-08-01\\n**Status:** Accepted\\n\\n### Context\\n\\nFed-Dup is a community project intended for broad adoption.\\n\\n### Decision\\n\\nLicense under the MIT License.\\n\\n### Consequences\\n\\n**Positive:**\\n- Permissive — allows commercial and non-commercial use, modification, and\\n  redistribution.\\n- Simple and well-understood.\\n- Compatible with most other licenses.\\n- Encourages adoption and contribution.\\n\\n**Negative:**\\n- No copyleft — derivative projects need not share their source. This is\\n  acceptable for a utility tool.\\n\\n---\\n\\n*New ADRs will be appended as significant architectural decisions are made.*\\n", "file_path": "ADR.md"}'}}]}

It duplicates Git repositories from a source host to a backup\\ndestination host using true Git mirroring, providing an exact, ref-for-ref\\ncopy of each repository.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Problem\\n\\nDevelopers and organizations rely on Git hosting platforms (GitHub, GitLab,\\nBitbucket, Codeberg, Gitea) for their source code. While these platforms are\\ngenerally reliable, they are single points of failure. Account suspensions,\\nplatform outages, accidental deletions, policy changes, and geopolitical\\naccess restrictions can all cause loss of access to critical code. Existing\\nbackup solutions are either commercial (expensive, opaque), database-backed\\n(heavy, operationally complex), or require manual scripting (fragile,\\nunmaintained).\\n\\n## Solution\\n\\nFed-Dup provides a simple, self-hosted, database-free alternative:\\n\\n- **True mirroring** — `git clone --mirror` + `git push --mirror` ensures the\\n  destination is an exact copy of the source, including all branches, tags,\\n  notes, and pull-request refs.\\n- **Database-free** — all state lives in a single `config.json` file. No\\n  database to install, manage, or back up.\\n- **Web UI** — a Streamlit dashboard for managing repositories, editing\\n  credentials, and triggering syncs with live progress feedback.\\n- **Background worker** — a continuous loop for always-on deployments, plus a\\n  `--once` mode for cron jobs and CI pipelines.\\n- **Token-safe** — credentials are injected at runtime and redacted from all\\n  error messages and logs.\\n- **Docker-ready** — one-command deployment with healthchecks and persistent\\n  volumes.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Key Features\\n\\n1. **Two-phase mirror sync** — clone/update then push, for exact fidelity.\\n2. **Multi-host support** — GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket, Codeberg, Gitea as\\n   sources; any HTTPS Git host as destination.\\n3. **Streamlit web UI** — add/remove repos, edit settings, sync with progress\\n   bar, view dashboard metrics.\\n4. **Background worker** — `auto_sync_loop()` or `--once` for cron/CI.\\n5. **Token redaction** — `_redact()` scrubs tokens from all output.\\n6. **URL sanitization** — strips credentials, preserves `https://`, ensures\\n   `.git` suffix.\\n7. **Forward-compatible config** — missing keys backfilled from defaults.\\n8. **Docker + Compose** — slim image, healthcheck, named volumes.\\n9. **16 CI/CD workflows** — build, test, lint, release, publish, CodeQL,\\n   Scorecards, Dependabot, stale, labeler, greetings, dependency review.\\n10. **Comprehensive documentation** — 25+ docs files covering installation,\\n    usage, deployment, security, contributing, roadmap, and more.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Technical Stack\\n\\n| Component       | Technology                          |\\n|-----------------|-------------------------------------|\\n| Language        | Python 3.11+
